<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-24ECA0A4-CA46-4E35-AE55-0CC8E4DC839A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Divide: $108 \div 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 108 \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {108} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {27 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {27 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
&= 27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 108 \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {108} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {27 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {27 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
&= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-089B56B3-5FE5-42D9-A2A5-5A1136AECEE9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the cost of downloading $5$ apps if each one costs $\pounds 2.98$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of downloading five apps}        &= 2.98 \times 5 \\\\
                                            &= \pounds 14.90
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 14.90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of downloading five apps}        &= 2.98 \times 5 \\\\
                                            &= \pounds 14.90
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 14.90$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Work out the sum of: $9, 99, 999$ and $9999$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
          &     &     &     &  9 \\
          &     &     &  9  &  9 \\
          &     &  9  &  9  &  9 \\
{}+       &  9  &  9  &  9  &  9 \\
\hline
       1  &  1  &  1  &  0  &  6
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11106$

</div>
</div>

          &     &     &  9  &  9 \\
          &     &  9  &  9  &  9 \\
{}+       &  9  &  9  &  9  &  9 \\
\hline
       1  &  1  &  1  &  0  &  6
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DEF306C6-4B02-40CC-97C6-0C1E0966876A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Divide $3$ by $0.5$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Dividing fractions is the same as multiplying by the reciprocal (inverse) of the second fraction.


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \div 0.5 \\\\
&= 3 \div \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
&= 3 \times \dfrac {10} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 10} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_divide_by_fraction_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \div 0.5 \\\\
&= 3 \div \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
&= 3 \times \dfrac {10} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 10} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7AC30911-BDA6-4864-8ACB-65F3B1D630DF'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

There is an even chance that I will pick a toffee from a large bag of sweets containing toffees, jellies and chocolates.  
If the bag contains $17$ jellies and $8$ chocolates, how many toffees are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

It will be even chance to select a toffee or jellies and chocolates if the number of toffee is equal to number of jellies and chocolates.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Number of toffee}   &= 17 + 8 \\\\
                          &= 25
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


Let the number of toffee $= p$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {p} {25 + p}  &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
2 \times p           &= 25 + p \\\\
2p                   &= 25 + p \\\\
2p - p               &= 25 \\\\
p                    &= 25
\end {aligned}
$

Number of toffee $= 25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>It will be even chance to select a toffee or jellies and chocolates if the number of toffee is equal to number of jellies and chocolates.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Number of toffee}   &= 17 + 8 \\\\
                          &= 25
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
Let the number of toffee $= p$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {p} {25 + p}  &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
2 \times p           &= 25 + p \\\\
2p                   &= 25 + p \\\\
2p - p               &= 25 \\\\
p                    &= 25
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of toffee $= 25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-651FA161-719C-4CEA-8415-BD9C2BEF8759'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Pens cost $7 \text{p}$ each and pencils cost $5 \text{p}$ each.  I buy some pens and pencils which cost $55 \text{p}$ exactly.

How many of each did I buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pens $= a$

and the number of pencils $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
7a + 5b       &= 55
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.

| $a$   |  $b$  |  $7a + 5b$  |
|:-----:|:-----:|------------:|
|$4$    | $6$   |   58        |
|$4$    | $5$   |   53        |
|$5$    | $4$   |   55        |

A quick check shows that $a = 5$ and $b = 4$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pens}       &= 5 \\\\
\text{Number of pencils}    &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$ pens and $4$ pencils

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pens $= a$
<emptyline>
and the number of pencils $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7a + 5b       &= 55
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.
<emptyline>
| $a$   |  $b$  |  $7a + 5b$  |
|:-----:|:-----:|------------:|
|$4$    | $6$   |   58        |
|$4$    | $5$   |   53        |
|$5$    | $4$   |   55        |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $a = 5$ and $b = 4$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pens}       &= 5 \\\\
\text{Number of pencils}    &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$ pens and $4$ pencils
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-17EBE603-FE66-4DF1-A566-229E356FE83D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Use the fact that $96815 \times 123 = 11908245$ to work out $96816  \times 123$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 96816 \times 123 \\\\
&= (96815 + 1) \times 123 \\\\
&= (96815 \times 123) + (1 \times 123) \\\\
&= 11908245 + 123 \\\\
&= 11908368
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11908368$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 96816 \times 123 \\\\
&= (96815 + 1) \times 123 \\\\
&= (96815 \times 123) + (1 \times 123) \\\\
&= 11908245 + 123 \\\\
&= 11908368
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11908368$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1A11854F-0304-4DF8-AF45-682DB61E74B6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Fill in the spaces with one of $+, - , \times, \div$  to make each statement correct

$8  \ \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore} \  2 = 5 \ \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore} \ 2$

$4  \ \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}  \ 3 = 60 \ \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore} \ 5$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \green
{+} 2 &= 5 \green
{\times} 2 \\\\
10 &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \green
{\times} 3 &= 60 \green
{\div} 5 \\\\
12 &= 12
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

+, $\times$


</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\times$, $\div$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
8 \rtq_katex_color_answer{+} 2 &= 5 \rtq_katex_color_answer{\times} 2 \\\\
10 &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
4 \rtq_katex_color_answer{\times} 3 &= 60 \rtq_katex_color_answer{\div} 5 \\\\
12 &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DC34D83A-C655-4169-BD7D-AF9298939526'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The three-digit number shown below can be divided by $3$ without a remainder. 

Fill in the box to show the largest possible value that the last digit could be.

$\boxed{5} \ \boxed{3} \ \boxed{ \phantom{5}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

7

Lets verify,

For the number to be divisible by 3, the sum of digits has to be divisible by 3.

Therefore maximum possible sum $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

5

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>7
<emptyline>
Lets verify,
<emptyline>
For the number to be divisible by 3, the sum of digits has to be divisible by 3.
<emptyline>
Therefore maximum possible sum $= 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>5
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7B74815D-732E-4E67-B878-86DB82735EBA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

At a busy railway station trains leave from platform $5$ every $6 \ \text{minutes}$ and from platform $8$ every 
$10 \ \text{minutes}$. Trains leave from both platforms at $15{:}57$. When do trains next leave both platforms at the same time?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time trains leave both platforms simultaneously }     &= 15{:}57 + 30 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                                            &= 16{:}27 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$ 16{:}27 $

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time trains leave both platforms simultaneously }     &= 15{:}57 + 30 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                                            &= 16{:}27 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$ 16{:}27 $
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-74930A6F-A20D-49EC-9CE0-3CBD9F1EAF84'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The price of a one-day ticket to a theme park is $\pounds 37.60$. Joe buys a ticket and experiences $8$ 
rides during the day. Work out the average cost per ride.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {37.6 \times 10} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {376} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 47} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 47} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {47} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {37.6 \times 10} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {376} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 47} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 47} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {47} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rishi pays an additional $\pounds 31$ for a special pass which gives front of line access to all rides throughout the day. 
He experiences $14$ rides. Does the special pass provide value for money? Give reasons for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using the values from the answer above,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 14 tickets}       &= 14 \times 4.7 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 65.8 \\\\
\text{Money saved}              &= 65.8 - 31 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 34.8
\end{aligned}
$

Yes, the special pass provides value for money as Rishi saved $\pounds 34.8$ in it

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 34.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using the values from the answer above,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 14 tickets}       &= 14 \times 4.7 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 65.8 \\\\
\text{Money saved}              &= 65.8 - 31 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 34.8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Yes, the special pass provides value for money as Rishi saved $\pounds 34.8$ in it
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 34.8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-12D28059-3C99-4BDF-BF66-584ACAC9EA71'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Catchpole gives the following instructions to her Year $6$ class:

-  Think of a number. 
-  Subtract $13$ from this number to get the first result. 
-  Go back to the original number and subtract 5 to get the second result. 
-  Multiply the first and second results together to get the final answer.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Savan follows Mrs Catchpole's instructions starting with the number $23$. Work out Savan's final answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= 10 \times 18 \\\\
&= 180
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$180$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= 10 \times 18 \\\\
&= 180
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$180$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Richard obtains a final answer of $0$. What number might he have thought of? Give all possibilities.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$(a - 13) \times (a - 5)  = 0$

$a = 13$

or 

$a = 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$ or $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$(a - 13) \times (a - 5)  = 0$
<emptyline>
$a = 13$
<emptyline>
or 
<emptyline>
$a = 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13$ or $5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

If it is a cold rainy day then I wear a raincoat. If it is a warm rainy day then I carry an umbrella. 
If I wear my raincoat then I also wear either gloves or a hat but not both. 
If I carry an umbrella I always wear gloves and sometimes wear my hat as well. 
I never carry an umbrella and wear my raincoat on the same day. 
For each of the following, state whether it is definitely true, false or uncertain.

<div class='question question'>

A sunflower seed takes a week to germinate after it has been sown in the ground. After germination it grows the same number centimetres each day.

Maya sows a sunflower seed. She measures the height of the plant at midday on the $16 \text{th}$ 
August and again at midday on the $21 \text{st}$ August. These
two measurements are recorded as $48 \ \text{cm}$ and $68 \ \text{cm}$ respectively.

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Ginny runs round and round the perimeter of the playground in a clockwise direction. 
She starts at the bottom left-hand corner labelled A on the diagram. Given that she runs a total distance of $1900 \ \text{m}$, draw a cross on the diagram to show where she finishes her run.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of turn she takes of the rectangle}                  &= \dfrac {1900} {250} \\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {19 \times 25 \times 4} {25 \times 10} \\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel 25 \times 4} {\cancel 25 \times10} \\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {10}\\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {76} {10} \\\\
                                                                  &= 7.6 \\\\                                                                  
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Point where Ginny will stop}                                &= 0.6 \times 250 \\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {6 \times 25 \times 10} {10} \\\\
                                                                  &= \dfrac {6 \times 25 \times \cancel 10} {\cancel 10} \\\\
                                                                  &= 6 \times 25 \\\\
                                                                  &= 150  \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

She finishes her run at point $150$ m away from A

<div class='question question'>

Once upon a time, in the Ancient Wizarding World of Haberdasher's:

One pound $= 20 \ \text{shillings}$ 

One shilling $= 12 \ \text{pence}$

A large Margarine-beer costs $1 \ \text{pound} \ 4 \ \text{shillings}$ and $9 \ \text{pence}$. 

A small Margarine-beer costs $14 \ \text{shillings}$ and $5 \ \text{pence}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the difference in price between a small and large beer. Give your answer in shillings and pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required


As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 20 \ \text {shillings} + 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 24 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 10 \ \text {shillings} \ 4 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text {shillings} \ 4 \text {p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 20 \ \text {shillings} + 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 24 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} - 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= 10 \ \text {shillings} \ 4 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 \ \text {shillings} \ 4 \text {p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the total cost of one large and two small beers. Give your answer in pounds, shillings and pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}  &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} + 2 \times 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} + 28 \ \text {shillings} \ 10 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 \ 32 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 + \pounds 1 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 2 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}  &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} + 2 \times 14 \ \text {shillings} \ 5 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 \ 4 \ \text {shillings} \ 9 \text {p} + 28 \ \text {shillings} \ 10 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 \ 32 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1 + \pounds 1 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p} \\\\
                    &= \pounds 2 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2 \ 12 \ \text {shillings} \ 19 \text {p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BA362291-5D3D-4A14-B988-DD3D44D7753B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Helen works out the width of the base and the area of each of the following four shapes and then draws a corresponding blob showing these measurements on a graph. 

Label four of the blobs on the graph with the letters $A, B, C$ and $D$. 

Draw a possible right-angled triangle, $E$, which corresponds to the remaining blob on the graph.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of A}        &= 12 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 48 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of A}   &= 2 \times (12 + 4) \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 16 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text{unit} \\\\
\text{Area of B}        &= (8 \times 3) + (8 \times 3) \\\\
                        &= 24 + 24 \\\\
                        &= 48 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of B}   &= 11 \times 2 + 8 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 22 + 16 \\\\
                        &= 38 \ \text {unit} \\\\
\text{Area of C}        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 2 \times 2 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {\cancel 2} \times \cancel 2 \times 2 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 16 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of C}   &= 4 + 8 + \sqrt{16+ 64}\\\\
                        &= 12 + \sqrt{80} \ \text {unit} \\\\
\text{Area of D}        &= (2 \times 6) + (2 \times 4) + (2 \times 6) \\\\
                        &= 12 + 8 + 12 \\\\
                        &= 32\ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of B}   &= 10 \times 2 + 6 \times 2  \\\\
                        &= 20 + 12 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text {unit} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of A}        &= 12 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 48 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of A}   &= 2 \times (12 + 4) \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 16 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text{unit} \\\\
\text{Area of B}        &= (8 \times 3) + (8 \times 3) \\\\
                        &= 24 + 24 \\\\
                        &= 48 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of B}   &= 11 \times 2 + 8 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 22 + 16 \\\\
                        &= 38 \ \text {unit} \\\\
\text{Area of C}        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 2 \times 2 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {\cancel 2} \times \cancel 2 \times 2 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 16 \ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of C}   &= 4 + 8 + \sqrt{16+ 64}\\\\
                        &= 12 + \sqrt{80} \ \text {unit} \\\\
\text{Area of D}        &= (2 \times 6) + (2 \times 4) + (2 \times 6) \\\\
                        &= 12 + 8 + 12 \\\\
                        &= 32\ \text {square unit} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of B}   &= 10 \times 2 + 6 \times 2  \\\\
                        &= 20 + 12 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text {unit} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-EFB9945E-41A3-4ED9-8B98-1E7688CB0B4D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows the one-way cycle paths in a park.

Work out the total number of possible routes to go:

-  from $A$ to $C$
-  from $A$ to $D$
-  from $A$ to $E$

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

 
If we go from $A$ to $C$, the possible arrangements are:

- A $1$ B $1$ C
- A $1$ B $2$ C
- A $2$ B $1$ C
- A $2$ B $2$ C
- A $3$ B $1$ C
- A $3$ B $2$ C

If we go from $A$ to $D$, the possible arrangements are:

If we use path $1$ between A and B, and $1$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 1 & B & 1 & C & 1 & D \\ 
A & 1 & B & 1 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 1 & B & 1 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 1 & B & 1 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

Count $= 4$

If we use path $1$ between A and B, and $2$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 1 & B & 2 & C & 1 & D \\
A & 1 & B & 2 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 1 & B & 2 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 1 & B & 2 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

Count $= 4$

If we use path $2$ between A and B, and $1$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 2 & B & 1 & C & 1 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 1 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 1 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 1 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

Count $= 4$

If we use path $2$ between A and B, and $2$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 2 & B & 2 & C & 1 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 2 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 2 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 2 & B & 2 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

If we use path $3$ between A and B, and $1$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 3 & B & 1 & C & 1 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 1 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 1 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 1 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

Count $= 4$

If we use path $3$ between A and B, and $2$ between B and c

$
\begin{matrix}
A & 3 & B & 2 & C & 1 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 2 & C & 2 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 2 & C & 3 & D \\
A & 3 & B & 2 & C & 4 & D \\
\end{matrix}
$

Count $= 4$

If we use path $4$ 

- A $4$ D

Count $= 1$

Total count $= 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 1 = 25$

If we go from $A$ to $E$, the possible arrangements are:

- $ABCDE$
- $ABCE$
- $ADE$
- $AE$

Count $= 4$

Total count $= 4 + 4 + 4 = 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A sequence of numbers which get multiplied (or divided) by a fixed amount each time is called a geometric progression $(GP)$. 
For example, the sequence:

$1 \text{st}$ term $= 6, \quad 2 \text{nd}$ term $= 12, \quad 3 \text{rd}$ term $= 24, \quad 4 \text{th}$ term $= 48$ 

is a $GP$ because you multiply by $2$ to go from one term to the next.

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-66E82DE8-5928-4039-A1D7-0B0410BA4486'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Snow White and the seven dwarves work in a mining company. 
As Chief Executive, Snow White earns as much as all the dwarves put together. 
Sneezy gets $24$ gold galleons a day. Sleepy earns $50\%$ more than Happy. 
Bashful earns twice as much as Grumpy who earns one more galleon than Sneezy. 
Doc gets $24$ galleons a day more than Dopey and between them they get four times that of Sneezy. 
Sleepy earns seven-eighths of Sneezy's salary.

Complete the table below to work out how much Snow White earns in a day.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Bashful Doc Dopey Grumpy Happy Sleepy Sneezy Snow White Gold Galleons Snow White

</div>
